tbmm-2020-07-09sit05spe0325par0096	Mr. President, the Court, the Regents, I've just tried to explain to you why it's contrary to the Constitution of Article 135. Article 8: The quality of the public institution is a single procedure in subject and location of the public corporate personality. There can't be more than one public corporate personality in the same place and the same subject. The goal-car relationship here is: to build a multi-bar, if democratic governance was the goal, it could be achieved by changing the electoral system, which cannot be achieved by multi-bar. However, if the goal is multi-bar, the goal is not to be achieved through the law, the constitutional amendment should be made, the constitutional amendments must be made. Now, out of 135, the second: Multiple bars are against the principle of fair trial. The principle of fair trial is not just one of the seven principles of fair trial if the barmen represent defence in the profession of attorney. It includes the whole principle of a fair trial, the right to a court, the principle of equality of weapons, an independent and impartial court, the principle of open procedure and quickness, the implementation of judicial decisions, the co-op, the right to innocence, the right to defend, without defense. In this regard, it violates the principle of a fair trial. We can determine that by acting on the 1.5th article of the Law of Law. Because it's based on the principle of independent defense and freedom. Just as the judge is based on the principle of independence and neutrality, the prosecutor represents security and the lawyer represents the principle of independence and autonomy. Therefore, without an independent and autonomous legal organisation, judicial independence is difficult or impossible. An important problem here is not primarily for today, but for the future. Because the division of the bar in this way -- for example, the establishment of 24 barons in Istanbul -- would be a risk of putting students in a course of education, starting from law school, without the purpose of law school -- between law school students and barrooms -- to form a political cliental clientist relationship. In this regard, the first problem will be political, religious and ideological dissolutions in the multi-bar organisation. When lawyers are given the opportunity to form a bar of their own free will, they will meet on the basis of political, philosophical, and religious measures -- which are decisive in their lives -- whether they want to or not. The second problem is that a bar formed with 2,000 members will enter into a political clientist relationship with law school students to increase the number of members of the existing bar -- probably because it has taken power from a party or a congregation. The heavy result of this is that the dissolutions, which began during the period of studenthood, according to political, religious and ideology, will create the appearance of a dissociative lawyer, and will not be possible to contribute to the defence, the ruling trilogy, which created the process of expressing what was right and true. In accordance with the proposed regulation, legal formation and multi-bar creation, the Law of Lawyership is contrary to Article 1, as well as to the right to a fair trial. This is not only against the right to a fair trial -- Article 2 of the Constitution -- but also against the democratic state of law. Indeed, if the Constitutional Court has been given the election, which is the basic rule of democracy in the formation of any institution, it must also acknowledge that its governance and functioning cannot violate democratic rules. In this regard, the principle of unequal representation is completely inconsistencies, and contrary to the 13th, 67th, and 135th articles of the Constitution, especially the 2nd article of the Constitution.
